<td valign="top"><strong>Question:</strong> Which statement best describes the enzyme&#x27;s divalent-cation cofactor behavior?<br><br><strong>Choices:</strong><br><br><strong>A.</strong> Mg2+ supports activity, but Co2+ and Mn2+ do not.<br><br><strong>B.</strong> Ni2+ supports activity about as effectively as Mg2+.<br><br><strong>C.</strong> Mg2+ and Co2+ support activity equally in vitro; Mn2+ is less effective, and Ni2+ is not accepted.<br><br><strong>D.</strong> Mn2+ is more effective than Mg2+, while Co2+ is not accepted.<br><br><strong>Flash-Next response:</strong><br><br><br><br>1. reasoning: The protein sequence contains characteristic motifs of an RNA-dependent RNA polymerase (RdRP), specifically showing the &quot;TDGKK&quot; motif and &quot;GDTVG&quot; motif, which are hallmarks of the GDD family of viral RNA polymerases (likely a picornavirus 3D polymerase based on sequence length ~360 aa and motif arrangement).<br><br>For picornavirus RNA-dependent RNA polymerases (such as poliovirus 3D polymerase), the divalent cation cofactor behavior has been well characterized through in vitro biochemical studies:<br><br>- **Mg²⁺** is the natural/physiological cofactor and supports full enzymatic activity.<br>- **Co²⁺** can substitute for Mg²⁺ and supports activity about as effectively as Mg²⁺ in vitro.<br>- **Mn²⁺** supports activity but is less effective than Mg²⁺ (often leading to altered fidelity).<br>- **Ni²⁺** is not accepted as a cofactor and does not support catalytic activity.<br><br>Evaluating each option:<br>- **A** is incorrect because Co²⁺ and Mn²⁺ do support activity (to varying degrees).<br>- **B** is incorrect because Ni²⁺ does not support activity effectively.<br>- **C** correctly describes the cofactor hierarchy: Mg²⁺ ≈ Co²⁺ &gt; Mn²⁺ &gt;&gt; Ni²⁺ (inactive).<br>- **D** is incorrect because Mn²⁺ is not more effective than Mg²⁺, and Co²⁺ IS accepted.<br><br>2. answer: C</td>
<td valign="top"><strong>Question:</strong> Beyond classifying the enzyme as divalent-cation-dependent, which specific pattern best describes its cation support?<br><br><strong>Choices:</strong><br><br><strong>A.</strong> Mg²⁺ and Co²⁺ support activity equally in vitro; Mn²⁺ is less effective, and Ni²⁺ is not accepted.<br><br><strong>B.</strong> Mg²⁺ supports activity, but Co²⁺ and Mn²⁺ do not; Ni²⁺ is accepted.<br><br><strong>C.</strong> Mg²⁺ and Mn²⁺ support activity equally; Co²⁺ is less effective, and Ni²⁺ is not accepted.<br><br><strong>D.</strong> Co²⁺ supports activity, but Mg²⁺, Mn²⁺, and Ni²⁺ are not accepted.<br><br><strong>Skills:</strong> cofactor_0098:2<br><br><strong>Flash-Next response:</strong><br><br><br><br>1. reasoning: The protein sequence contains characteristic motifs of a prenyltransferase, including the DGxxD motif (seen in &quot;MDGKKYDFP&quot; and &quot;GDTVGAGDS&quot;) which is the hallmark of protein farnesyltransferase/geranylgeranyltransferase catalytic subunits.
